## Alex sequencing decision captured at 2026-04-24T03:34:08Z

Decision:
- Adopt Alex's recommended opening recovery slice as the default P0 execution order.
- Treat watcher `#207`, `#210`, `#212`, `#213` as existing substrate, not the first build target.
- Pull watcher durability `#208`, `#209`, `#214` into the opening tranche only if Mike's validation says current delivery guarantees are not trustworthy enough for recovery.

Opening recovery tranche:
1. Slice A: orch transaction + transition base `#178`, `#179`, `#180`, `#184`, `#244`
2. Slice B: event durability + fan-out `#182`, `#234`, `#235`, `#236`, `#237`

## Mike reliability synthesis captured at 2026-04-24T03:39:29Z

Decision:
- Reframe the opening P0 slice around one end-to-end cold-worker canary, not general concurrency recovery.
- Hold retry/spawn churn to a single canary lane until one worker proves `spawn -> auth/prompt injection -> first claude turn -> watchdog push -> orch transition -> watcher/audit visibility`.
- Treat worker-path fail-closed checks as the first reliability gate: dual-label contract, subscription creds mount, prompt mount, and writable `/bare`.

Operational consequences:
- Existing failed-worker backlog should be drained or quarantined instead of recycled into dispatch.
- Alex's tranche order remains the macro recovery frame, but the first execution package must now be cut around boundary-contract validation plus delivery visibility.
- Watcher durability stays tied to recovery trustworthiness: if current delivery/audit signals cannot prove the canary path, watcher durability work moves into the opening slice rather than after it.

Priority adjustments:
- `#54` resource caps move up from deferred hardening to return-to-service gate. Reopening dispatch without caps risks another thrash loop.
- `#255` containerization parity remains a live reliability blocker because repo/path/protocol drift makes recovery environment-dependent.
- `#49` / `#50` backup-restore remain important but are not the immediate gate to restoring one clean worker path.

## P0 escalation recorded at 2026-04-24T18:27:47Z

Decision:
- Accept Mike's report on task `6b6eb427` as the active P0 coordination item.
- Classify the failure as merger orchestration/reconciler behavior, not CanaryV17 task-content failure.
- Halt further confidence in canary approvals until the loop condition is addressed or explicitly contained.

- Parent repeatedly reaches `testing_ready`, enters `merging`, merger exits immediately with code 128, and reconciler logic requeues it through `in_progress -> testing_ready`.

- A burst of five merger containers in roughly 22 seconds indicates retry inside merge dispatch or worker lifecycle.

Ownership:
- Seth owns the technical fix directive: stop requeue after exhausted merger attempts, preserve task-correlated merger evidence, and define merger preflight/per-attempt telemetry before further canary approvals.
- Mike owns operational containment and evidence preservation until the fix path is clear.
